Table 2:
Clustering error (CE), cluster mean error (CME), cluster covariance error (COVME), true positive rate (TPR) and false positive rate (FPR) of four methods with varying cluster mean parameter
\mu
and cluster covariance parameter
\nu
.
HECM
is the proposed algorithm;
Kmeans
applies K-means clustering directly to the vectorized tensor samples;
DTC
is proposed by
Sun and Li 2019
; and
DEEM
is proposed by
Mai et al. 2021
.

We fix
n=400
and set
\mu=0.8,0.85
and
\nu=0.3,0.6
. Table
2
reports the mean evaluation criteria with the standard errors in the parentheses, based on 50 data replications. Since
Kmeans
and
DTC
do not give estimates for the covariance matrices or precision matrices directly, we first obtain cluster membership from these algorithms and then estimate the covariance within each cluster. As
Kmeans
,
DTC
and
DEEM
do not consider the sparsity for the covariance matrices or precision matrices, the TPR and FPR are not reported for these three methods.
Our proposed
HECM
method is seen to achieve the best performance among all competing methods, in terms of both estimation accuracy
and clustering accuracy. We see that clustering errors (CE) from all three methods decrease as
\mu
increases, as the cluster centers become more separated when
\mu
is large. The clustering performance from
Kmeans
is very sensitive to correlation strength
\nu
, as the standard K-means algorithm treats the data space as isotropic
(
Hao et al. 2017
)
. When the covariance matrix in the mixture model is non-diagonal, the distribution within each cluster is highly non-spherical. In this case, the K-means algorithm is expected to produce an unsatisfactory clustering result.
It is worth noting that the
HECM
enjoys a good performance even when its initialization calculated from
Kmeans
performs poorly.
For example, when
n=400
,
\mu=0.8
and
\nu=0.6
, the CE from
Kmeans
is 0.365 and it is reduced to 0.132 for
HECM
.
The
DTC
method does not account for correlations among variables and it assumes a different statistical model than ours (see discussions in Section
1
). Therefore, its performance is not as competitive. For
DEEM
method, although it considers a tensor mean structure, it relies on a critical assumption that the discriminant tensors are sparse. We conjecture the unsatisfactory performance of
DEEM
when
\nu=0.3
is due to the model misspecification.

Figure 3:
Errors with varying sample size
n
from the
HECM
method. The left panel shows errors from estimating the cluster means (CME) and the right panel shows errors from estimating the cluster covariances (COVME). The “
\times
” marks the mean error for each setting.

Finally, we note that the cluster mean error (CME) decreases as
\mu
increases, which agrees with Theorem
2
as a larger
\mu
implies a larger
\omega_{\min}
. Furthermore, in Figure
3
we show the empirical error rates (red dotted line) of the proposed
HECM
with varying sample sizes when
\mu=0.85
and
\nu=0.3
. Clearly, the estimation errors of
HECM
decrease as
n
increases and the empirical error rates for both CME and COVME align well with the theoretical rate of
n^{-1/2}
, while all other model parameters are fixed. These again agree with our theoretical result in Theorem
2
.

7
Real Data Analysis

In this section, we apply our proposed method to a brain connectivity analysis using resting-state functional magnetic resonance imaging (fMRI). The data are from the Autism Brain Imaging Data Exchange
(
Di Martino et al. 2014
, ABIDE;)
, a study of autism spectrum disorder (ASD).
The ABIDE data were obtained from multiple imaging sites. We choose to focus on the fMRI data from the University of Utah School of Medicine (USM) site, since the sample size is relatively large (
n=57
) meanwhile not too large to apply K-means clustering to the vectorized data for comparison. The data at the USM site consist of the resting-state fMRI from 57 subjects with 22 ASD subjects and 35 normal controls.
For each subject, the fMRI data are preprocessed into a third-order tensor
\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{116\times 116\times N}
where
N
is the number of temporal windows. More details of data preparation are included in Section
F
.

Clustering errors from

,

and

in the ABIDE data.

We cluster the subjects using the proposed
HECM
algorithm and then compare the estimated clustering result with each subject’s diagnosis status, which is treated as the true label in this analysis. For a fair comparison, we fix the number of clusters as
K=2
in all methods and compare the clustering results with the true diagnosis status. We report the clustering error of our method in Table
3
, along with errors from
Kmeans
,
DTC
and
DEEM
. It is seen that
HECM
outperforms the
Kmeans
and
DTC
as it gives a smaller clustering error.
For
DEEM
, the algorithm stops after one iteration and assigns all data points to one cluster, which gives a degenerate solution.
This issue persists even when we use the true label to initialize
DEEM
.
It is also interesting to see that both
HECM
and
DTC
give smaller errors when the number of windows increases from
N=1
to
N=30
. This gain in clustering accuracy when increasing the number of temporal windows suggests that the underlying brain connectivity in this study is likely time-varying rather than static.

8
Discussion

In this work, we consider a model-based clustering method that jointly models and clusters tensors using a probabilistic tensor mixture model, similar to
Balakrishnan et al. 2017
;
Yi and Caramanis 2015
. That is, we assume the tensors are from a mixture of tensor normal distributions.
If we consider a different data distribution, such as the t-distribution
(
Andrews et al. 2011
)
, then each step in the HECM algorithm needs to be re-calculated and the properties of conditional concavity, gradient stability, and statistical errors need to be re-verified with respect to the parameters in the new distributional assumption.

The derived theoretical results of the
HECM
algorithm hold regardless of the ordering in updating parameters in Algorithm
1
, as long as the initial parameter
{\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)}
satisfies the initialization condition. Specifically, the key step in our theoretical analysis is to establish properties of
Q_{n}(\bm{\vartheta}^{\prime},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\bm{\vartheta}}|{\bm{\Theta}})
and these properties are used to establish the one-step contraction in each of the conditional M-step update. Regardless of the updating order, these properties hold and the contraction can be guaranteed, as long as
\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\bm{\vartheta}}
is within a ball centered at
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}
. In our proof, we explicitly show that the updated parameters from the conditional M-step update still fall within the said ball around
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}
(Lemma
S16
-
S18
).
Therefore, given
{\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)}
satisfies the initialization condition, the specific ordering of the parameters in the prior updates does not affect the theoretical results.

Finally, in Theorem
1
and
2
, the sample splitting technique is needed in the sample level analysis.
In our setting, a uniform concentration analysis, which avoids the need for sample splitting, is extremely challenging, as our CM-step involves a sequence of conditional updates and our thus developed proof strategy requires a stronger result on the statistical error. The standard uniform concentration analysis of EM algorithms typically requires

\sup_{{\bm{\Theta}}\in\mathbb{B}_{r}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})}\|\nabla Q_{n}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\nabla Q({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}|{\bm{\Theta}})\|_{\mathcal{R}}\leq\epsilon_{n},

where
\mathcal{R}
is some pre-specified norm and
\mathbb{B}_{r}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
is a ball centered around
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}

(
Balakrishnan et al. 2017
)
.
In our proof, due to the sequence of conditional updates in the M-step, the sample-level analysis requires a stronger uniform concentration result as follows

\sup_{{\bm{\Theta}},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}\in\mathbb{B}_{r}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})}\|\nabla_{\bm{\vartheta}}Q_{n}(\bm{\vartheta}^{\prime},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\bm{\vartheta}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\nabla_{\bm{\vartheta}}Q(\bm{\vartheta}^{\prime},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\bm{\vartheta}}|{\bm{\Theta}})\|_{\mathcal{R^{\prime}}}\leq\epsilon_{n},

where
\bm{\vartheta}
is the parameter to be updated and
\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\bm{\vartheta}}
collects other parameters being conditioned and
\bm{{\bm{\Theta}}}
is the parameter used to calculate cluster probabilities
\tau_{ik}
’s.
It is challenging to bound
\nabla_{\bm{\vartheta}}Q_{n}(\bm{\vartheta}^{\prime},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\bm{\vartheta}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\nabla_{\bm{\vartheta}}Q(\bm{\vartheta}^{\prime},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\bm{\vartheta}}|{\bm{\Theta}})
uniformly over
{\bm{\Theta}},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}\in\mathbb{B}_{r}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
, as data
\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}
’s and parameters
{\bm{\Theta}},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}
are involved in a highly complicated density function due to the tensor low-rank and heterogeneous separable covariance structures, and the term
\nabla_{\bm{\vartheta}}Q_{n}(\bm{\vartheta}^{\prime},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\bm{\vartheta}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\nabla_{\bm{\vartheta}}Q(\bm{\vartheta}^{\prime},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\bm{\vartheta}}|{\bm{\Theta}})
cannot be formulated as separate functions of
\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}
’s and
{\bm{\Theta}},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}
.
We leave an in-depth investigation of this uniform concentration analysis as future work.

Supplementary Materials of “Jointly Modeling and Clustering Tensors in High Dimensions”

In the supplement, we first discuss the implementation details of the proposed algorithm, then state some important technical lemmas, followed by the detailed proofs of the main theorems and the proofs of the technical lemmas. We conclude with computational details of our main algorithm and additional results of simulations and real data analysis.

A
Implementation details

Update in the M-step.

Recall
\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m\in[M]}{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}={\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,1}\circ\cdots\circ{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,M}
and
\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m\in[M]}{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}={\bm{\Omega}}_{k,M}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,1}
.
Let
\bm{a}_{k,r,m}^{(t+1)}={\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits_{m^{\prime}<m}^{\circ}{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m^{\prime}}^{(t+1)}\circ\prod\limits_{m^{\prime}>m}^{\circ}{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m^{\prime}}^{(t)})}
,
\bm{b}_{k,r,m}^{(t+1)}=\sum\limits_{r^{\prime}<r}\left\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r^{\prime},m}^{(t+1)},{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{(t)}\right\rangle\omega_{k,r^{\prime}}^{(t+1)}

{\mathrm{vec}(\prod_{m^{\prime}\neq m}^{\circ}{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m^{\prime}}^{(t+1)})}+\bm{a}_{k,r,m}^{(t+1)}\omega_{k,r}^{(t)}+\sum\limits_{r^{\prime}>r}\left\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r^{\prime},m}^{(t)},{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{(t)}\right\rangle\omega_{k,r^{\prime}}^{(t)}{\mathrm{vec}(\prod_{m^{\prime}\neq m}^{\circ}{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m^{\prime}}^{(t)})}
and

n_{k}^{(t)}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})^{(t+1)}
. We use
{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}(j)
to denots the
j
-th element of
{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}
and
{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}(j,l)
to denote the
(j,l)
-th element of
{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}
. The unconstrained (i.e., without the unit-norm constraint) update of
{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}
is given in the following proposition with its proof delayed to Section
E13
.

Proposition 2
.

Let
\tilde{{\bm{\beta}}}_{k,r,m}^{(t+1)}=\arg\max_{{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}}\,Q_{n/T}({\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m},{\bm{\Theta}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)})-\lambda_{0}^{(t+1)}\left\|{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}\right\|_{1}
. We have, for each
k,r,m
,

\tilde{{\bm{\beta}}}_{k,r,m}^{(t+1)}(j)=\begin{cases}{\bm{g}}_{k,r,m}^{(t+1)}(j)-\frac{n\lambda^{(t+1)}_{0}\mathrm{sign}\left({\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{(t)}(j)\right)}{n_{k}^{(t)}C^{(t+1)}_{k,r,m}{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{(t)}(j,j)}&\mathrm{if}\,|{\bm{h}}_{k,r,m}^{(t+1)}(j)|>\lambda^{(t+1)}_{0},\\
0&\mathrm{otherwise,}\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle{\bm{g}}_{k,r,m}^{(t+1)}(j)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)})\tilde{{\bm{g}}}^{(t+1)}_{k,r,m}(j)}{n_{k}^{(t)}C^{(t+1)}_{k,r,m}{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{(t)}(j,j)}-\frac{\sum_{l=1}^{d_{m}}{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{(t)}(j,l){\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{(t)}(l)}{{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{(t)}(j,j)}+{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{(t)}(j),

\displaystyle{\bm{h}}_{k,r,m}^{(t+1)}(j)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}C^{(t)}_{k,r,m}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)})\tilde{{\bm{g}}}^{(t+1)}_{k,r,m}(j)-C^{(t)}_{k,r,m}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)})\sum_{l\neq j}{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{(t)}(j,l){\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{(t)}(l).

Here
\tilde{{\bm{g}}}^{(t)}_{k,r,m}={\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{(t)}\left({\bm{\mathcal{X}}}^{(t+1)}_{i,-r}\right)_{(m)}\left({\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m^{\prime}\neq m}{\bm{\Omega}}^{(t)}_{k,m^{\prime}}}\right)\bm{b}_{k,r,m}^{(t+1)}
,
C^{(t+1)}_{k,r,m}=\omega_{k,r}^{(t)}{\bm{a}_{k,r,m}^{(t+1)}}^{\top}\left({\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m^{\prime}\neq m}{\bm{\Omega}}^{(t)}_{k,m^{\prime}}}\right)\bm{b}_{k,r,m}^{(t+1)}
, and
{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}^{(t+1)}_{i,-r}=\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\sum\limits_{r^{\prime}<r}\omega^{(t+1)}_{k,r^{\prime}}\prod\limits_{m\in[M]}^{\circ}{\bm{\beta}}^{(t+1)}_{k,r^{\prime},m}-\sum\limits_{r^{\prime}>r}\omega^{(t)}_{k,r^{\prime}}\prod\limits_{m\in[M]}^{\circ}{\bm{\beta}}^{(t)}_{k,r^{\prime},m}
.

Update of
\eta_{k}
.
The update of
\eta_{k}
in (
13
) is obtained by

\displaystyle\eta_{k}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle=\arg\max_{\eta}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)})\log\{f_{k}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}|{\bm{\theta}}_{k})\}

\displaystyle=\arg\max_{\eta}\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)})\log\left(\left|\eta_{k}\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m\in[M]}\check{\bm{\Omega}}^{(t+1)}_{k,m}\right|\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)})\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{(t+1)}_{k})^{\top}\left(\eta_{k}\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m\in[M]}\check{\bm{\Omega}}^{(t+1)}_{k,m}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{(t+1)}_{k}).

By using the fact that
|c{\bm{A}}|=c^{d}|{\bm{A}}|
for a matrix
{\bm{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, we can simplify the optimization for
\eta_{k}
as follows:

\eta_{k}^{(t+1)}=\arg\max_{\eta_{k}}\,n_{k}^{(t)}d\log(\eta_{k})-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)})\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{(t+1)}_{k})^{\top}\eta_{k}\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m\in[M]}\check{\bm{\Omega}}^{(t+1)}_{k,m}\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{(t+1)}_{k}),

where
n_{k}^{(t)}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)})
. Setting the first derivative to zero, we obtain:

\eta_{k}^{(t+1)}=n_{k}^{(t)}d\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)})\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{(t+1)}_{k})^{\top}\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m\in[M]}\check{\bm{\Omega}}^{(t+1)}_{k,m}\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{(t+1)}_{k})\right\}^{-1}.

Initialization.
In Algorithm
1
, given the tuning parameters, we need to determine the initial values
\pi_{k}^{(0)}
,
{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{(0)}
,
\omega_{k,r}^{(0)}
and
{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{(0)}
for all
k,r
and
m
.
In our implementation, when
d_{1},\ldots,d_{m}
are moderate, we initialize the cluster label via
K
-means on the vectorized tensor observations
\{\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
to find
\{{\bm{z}}^{(0)}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
. We set
\pi_{k}^{(0)}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bf 1}({\bm{z}}^{(0)}_{i}=k)
,
k\in[K]
.
After that, we estimate
\omega^{(0)}_{k,r},{\bm{\beta}}^{(0)}_{k,r,m}
using the standard CP decomposition
(
Kolda and Bader 2009
)
on each
\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{(0)}_{k}=\frac{1}{n\pi_{k}^{(0)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bf 1}({\bm{z}}^{(0)}_{i}=k)\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}
.
We first let

\tilde{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{(0)}=\left\{\frac{1}{n\pi_{k}^{(0)}\sum_{i=1}^{n/T}}{\bf 1}({\bm{z}}^{(0)}_{i}=k)(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{(0)}_{k})_{(m)}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{(0)}_{k})_{(m)}^{\top}\right\}^{-1},

and the
\tilde{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{(0)}
’s are then normalized as in (
13
) to give
{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{(0)}
for all
k,m
.
In our experiments, this initialization leads to good numerical performances. A similar procedure was also considered in
Mai et al. 2021
.
When
d_{1},\ldots,d_{M}
are large, we may avoid the high computational cost from performing
K
-means on high-dimensional vectors and alternatively consider the tensor clustering method in
Sun and Li 2019
, which applies
K
-means on the output from tensor decomposition.

Stopping rule.

In Algorithm
1
, the maximum number of iterations
T
needs to be specified. In our implementation, we set
T=20
.
In practice, it is recommended to run Algorithm
1
when the distance between
{\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)}
and
{\bm{\Theta}}^{(t-1)}
becomes less than a pre-specified tolerance level.
The tolerance level is set to
10^{-4}
in our experiments and we find the algorithm usually converges within 10 iterations.

Parameter tuning.

The proposed Algorithm
1
involves a number of tuning parameters, including the number of mixtures
K
, the rank
R
and sparsity parameters
\lambda^{(t)}_{0}
’s,
\lambda^{(t)}_{1}
’s,
\ldots,\lambda^{(t)}_{M}
’s. To reduce computing cost, we recommend tuning these parameters sequentially. First, to select the number of mixtures
K
, we recommend fitting the model with different numbers of clusters
K
and selecting one using the Bayesian information criterion (BIC)
(
Raftery and Dean 2006
;
Mai et al. 2021
)
.

Keribin 2000
showed BIC is consistent in selecting the number of clusters in a mixture model and selecting
K
via BIC has been found to enjoy a good empirical performance when compared with other methods
(
Steele and Raftery 2010
)
. Moreover, having different parameters
\lambda^{(t)}_{0}
’s,
\lambda^{(t)}_{1}
’s,
\ldots,\lambda^{(t)}_{M}
’s in each iteration is due to theoretical considerations, as the estimation error, which determines the level of regularization, changes at each iteration.
Such an iterative regularization has also been considered in
Yi and Caramanis 2015
;
Mai et al. 2021
.
In practice, tuning for these parameters at each HECM iteration can significantly increase the computational cost.
For practical considerations, we fix
\lambda^{(t)}_{0}=\lambda_{0}
and
\lambda^{(t)}_{m}=\lambda_{m}
in our experiments.
We note that this simplification is commonly employed in high-dimensional EM algorithms
(
Mai et al. 2021
, e.g.,)
and is found to give a satisfactory performance in our experiments. To tune
R
,
\lambda_{0}
and
\lambda_{m}
’s, we consider minimizing the following extended BIC selection criterion
(
Chen and Chen 2008
)
,

\text{eBIC}=-2\,\log\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{k}f_{k}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}|{\bm{\theta}}_{k})\right\}+\left\{\log(n)+\frac{1}{2}\log(p_{{\bm{\Theta}}})\right\}s_{\text{tot}},

(A1)

where
p_{{\bm{\Theta}}}
is the total number of parameters in
{\bm{\Theta}}
and
s_{\text{tot}}
is the total number of non-zero parameters for a given
{\bm{\Theta}}
.
To further speed up the computation, we tune parameters
R
,
\lambda_{0}
and
\lambda_{m}
’s sequentially. That is, among the set of values for
R
,
\lambda_{0}
,
\lambda_{m}
’s, we first tune
R
while
\lambda_{0}
,
\lambda_{m}
’s are fixed at their minimum values. Given the selected
R
, we then tune
\lambda_{0}
while
\lambda_{m}
’s are fixed at its minimum. Finally, given the selected
R
,
\lambda_{0}
, we tune
\lambda_{m}
’s. Such a sequential tuning procedure enjoys a good performance and is commonly employed in high dimensional problems
(
Danaher et al. 2014
;
Sun and Li 2019
;
Chi et al. 2020
;
Zhou et al. 2023
)
.

B
Technical Lemmas

B1
Key technical lemmas

Next, we introduce several key technical lemmas used in the proof of Theorem
1
-
2
.
The proofs of Lemmas
1b
-
8b
are delayed to Sections
D1
-
D3
,
D4
-
D8
, respectively.

We start with some new notation. Write
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\ast})=\left\{{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}\mid\frac{\left\|{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}-{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\ast}\right\|_{2}}{\sigma_{\min}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\ast})}\leq\alpha\right\}
and
c_{\alpha}=\left(\frac{C_{0}\omega_{\min}}{(R-1)\omega_{\max}}\right)^{\frac{1}{M-1}}
, where
C_{0}
is as defined in Condition
2
. Define the population Q-function
Q({\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}|{\bm{\Theta}})
as

Q({\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}|{\bm{\Theta}})=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})\{\log(\pi^{\prime}_{k})+\log f_{k}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}|{\bm{\theta}}^{\prime}_{k})\}\right].

(A2)

Let
\mathcal{P}_{1}({\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m})=\|{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}\|_{1}
,
\mathcal{P}_{2}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m})=\|{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}\|_{1,\text{off}}
and let
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\ast}
,
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\ast}
be the dual norms of
\mathcal{P}_{1}
,
\mathcal{P}_{2}
, respectively. It is important to note that the dependence of
Q_{n}(\bm{\vartheta}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-\bm{\vartheta}}|{\bm{\Theta}})
on
{\bm{\Theta}}
only comes via
\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})
.

Lemma 1b

(Restricted Strong Concavity for
Q_{n/T}
)
.

Suppose
R=1
and Conditions
1
and
3
hold. Let
{\bm{\Theta}},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}\in\mathcal{B}_{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
and satisfies
{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m},\bar{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}\in\mathcal{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\ast})
for all
k,m
.
For any
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}
and
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime\prime}
satisfying
{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime},{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\ast})
for all
k,m
, it holds with probability at least
1-1/\{\log(nd)\}^{2}
that,

\displaystyle{\displaystyle Q_{\frac{n}{T}}({\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-Q_{\frac{n}{T}}({\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\left\langle\nabla_{{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}Q_{\frac{n}{T}}({\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}}),{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime\prime}-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime}\right\rangle}

(A3)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2}\left\|{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime\prime}-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle Q_{\frac{n}{T}}(\omega_{k}^{\prime\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-\omega_{k}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-Q_{\frac{n}{T}}(\omega_{k}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-\omega_{k}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\left\langle\nabla_{\omega_{k}}Q_{\frac{n}{T}}(\omega_{k}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-\omega_{k}}|{\bm{\Theta}}),\omega_{k}^{\prime\prime}-\omega_{k}^{\prime}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime}}{2}\left|\omega_{k}^{\prime\prime}-\omega_{k}^{\prime}\right|^{2},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle Q_{\frac{n}{T}}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-Q_{\frac{n}{T}}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\left\langle\nabla_{{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}Q_{\frac{n}{T}}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}}),{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime\prime}-{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\gamma_{m}}{2}\left\|{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime\prime}-{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2},

where
\gamma_{0}=\frac{c_{0}}{4}\omega_{\min}^{2}(\phi_{1}/2)^{M}
,
\gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime}=c_{0}(\phi_{1}/2)^{M}
and
\gamma_{m}=c_{0}\frac{d}{d_{m}}(6\phi_{2})^{-2}
for some constant
c_{0}>0
.

Lemma 2b

(Gradient Stability for
Q
)
.

Suppose
R=1
and Condition
3
holds for
\gamma>0
. Let
{\bm{\Theta}},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}\in\mathcal{B}_{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
and satisfies
{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m},\bar{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}\in\mathcal{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\ast})
for all
k,m
.
For any
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
satisfying
{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\ast})
for all
k,m
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla_{{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}Q({\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\nabla_{{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}Q({\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\right\rVert_{2}\leq\tau_{0}\cdot\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}),

(A4)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla_{\omega_{k}}Q(\omega_{k}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-\omega_{k}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\nabla_{\omega_{k}}Q(\omega_{k}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-\omega_{k}}|{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\right\rVert_{2}\leq\tau_{0}^{\prime\prime}\cdot\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla_{{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}Q({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\nabla_{{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}Q({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\right\rVert_{\text{F}}\leq\tau_{1}\cdot\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}).

where
\tau_{0}=\frac{\gamma}{12\sqrt{K(R+1)(M+1)}}
,
\tau_{0}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{\gamma\omega_{\max}}{12\sqrt{K(R+1)(M+1)}}
and
\tau_{1}=\frac{\gamma d}{12\sqrt{K(R+1)(M+1)}}
.

Lemma 3b
.

Suppose
R=1
and Condition
1
and
4
hold. Let
{\bm{\Theta}},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}\in\mathcal{B}_{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
and satisfies
{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m},\bar{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}\in\mathcal{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\ast})
for all
k,m
.
For any
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
satisfying
{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\ast})
for all
k,m
, it holds with probability at least
1-K(2K+1)/\{\log(nd)\}^{2}
that

\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}Q_{n/T}({\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\nabla_{{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}Q({\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})\right\|_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\ast}}\leq c_{1}\omega_{\max}\sqrt{T\frac{\log d}{n}},

(A5)

with probability at least
1-K/\{\log(nd)\}^{2}
that

\left|\nabla_{\omega_{k}}Q_{n/T}(\omega_{k}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-\omega_{k}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\nabla_{\omega_{k}}Q(\omega_{k}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-\omega_{k}}|{\bm{\Theta}})\right|\leq c^{\prime\prime}_{1}\omega_{\max}\sqrt{T\frac{\log\log(nd)}{n}},

(A6)

and with probability at least
1-K(8K+2)/\{\log(nd)\}^{2}
that

\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}Q_{n/T}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\nabla_{{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}Q({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})\right\|_{\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\ast}}\leq c_{2}\frac{d}{d_{m}}\sqrt{T\frac{\log d}{n}},

(A7)

where
c_{1},c^{\prime\prime}_{1},c_{2}
are positive constants.

Lemma 4b

(One-step Contraction)
.

Suppose
R=1
and Conditions
1
-
4
hold with
\gamma d_{\max}\leq C_{1}
for some constant
C_{1}>0
.
Let
\lambda_{0}^{(1)}=4\epsilon_{0}+\tau_{0}\frac{\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})}{\sqrt{s_{1}}}
,
\lambda_{m}^{(1)}=4\epsilon_{m}+3\tau_{1}\frac{\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})}{2\sqrt{s_{2}+d_{m}}}
,
where
\tau_{0}
are
\tau_{1}
are as defined in (
A4
) and
\epsilon_{0}=c_{1}\omega_{\max}\sqrt{\log d\cdot T/n}
and
\epsilon_{m}=c_{2}({d}/{d_{m}})\sqrt{\log d\cdot T/n}
for
c_{1}
in (
A5
) and
c_{2}
in (
A7
).
The estimator of Algorithm
1
after one-step update satisfies with probability at least
1-C_{3}/\{\log(nd)\}^{2}
,

\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\\
\leq\rho\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})+\frac{C_{2}}{1-\rho}\left\{\frac{1}{\omega_{\min}}\sqrt{T\frac{s_{1}\log d}{n}}+\max_{m}\sqrt{T\frac{(s_{2}+d_{m})\log d}{nd_{m}}}\right\},

where
C_{2},C_{3}>0
are constants and
\rho
given in (
A82
) satisfies
0<\rho\leq 1/3
.

Lemma 5b
.

Suppose
R>1
and Conditions
1
and
3
hold. Let
{\bm{\Theta}},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}\in\mathcal{B}_{c_{\alpha}}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
and satisfies
{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m},\bar{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}\in\mathcal{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\ast})
for all
k,m
.
For any
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}
and
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime\prime}
, it holds with probability at least
1-1/\{\log(nd)\}^{2}
that,

\displaystyle Q_{n/T}({\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{\prime\prime},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-Q_{n/T}({\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{\prime},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})

(A8)

\displaystyle-\left\langle\nabla_{{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}}Q_{n/T}({\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{\prime},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}}),{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{\prime}-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{\ast}\right\rangle\leq-\frac{\gamma_{0}^{\prime}}{2}\left\|{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{\prime\prime}-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2},

where
\gamma_{0}^{\prime}=c_{0}(\phi_{1}/2)^{M-1}\left\{(1-c_{\alpha})^{2}\omega_{\min}^{2}-(1+c_{\alpha})^{2}\omega_{\max}^{2}(R-1)R(\xi+2c_{\alpha}+c_{\alpha}^{2})^{M+1}\right\}
.

Lemma 6b
.

Suppose
R>1
and Condition
3
holds for
\gamma>0
. Let
{\bm{\Theta}},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}\in\mathcal{B}_{c_{\alpha}}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
and satisfies
{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m},\bar{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}\in\mathcal{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\ast})
for all
k,m
. For any
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{c_{\alpha}}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla_{{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}}Q({\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{\prime},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\nabla_{{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}}Q({\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{\prime},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\right\rVert_{2}\leq\tau_{0}^{\prime}\cdot\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}),

(A9)

where
\tau_{0}^{\prime}=\{1+(R-1)(\xi+2c_{\alpha}+c_{\alpha}^{2})\}\tau_{0}
and
\tau_{0}
is as defined in (
A4
).

Lemma 7b
.

Suppose
R>1
and Condition
1
and Condition
4
hold. Let
{\bm{\Theta}},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}\in\mathcal{B}_{c_{\alpha}}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
and satisfies
{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m},\bar{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}\in\mathcal{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\ast})
for all
k,m
. For any
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{c_{\alpha}}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
, it holds with probability at least
1-K(2K+1)/\{\log(nd)\}^{2}
that

\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}}Q_{n/T}({\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{\prime},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})-\nabla_{{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}}Q({\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}^{\prime},\bar{\bm{\Theta}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,r,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})\right\|_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\ast}}\leq c^{\prime}_{1}\omega_{\max}\sqrt{T\frac{\log d}{n}},

(A10)

with
c_{1}^{\prime}
is some positive constant.

Lemma 8b
.

Suppose
R>1
, Conditions
1
-
4
hold with
\gamma d_{\max}\leq C_{1}
for some constant
C_{1}>0
and
R\xi^{M}\precsim(\log d)^{-1}
.
Let
\lambda_{0}^{(1)}=4\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}+\tau^{\prime}_{0}\frac{\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})}{\sqrt{s_{1}}}
,
\lambda_{m}^{(1)}=4\epsilon_{m}+3\tau_{1}\frac{\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})}{2\sqrt{s_{2}+d_{m}}}
, where
\tau^{\prime}_{0}
is as defined in (
A9
),
\epsilon_{0}^{\prime}=c^{\prime}_{1}\omega_{\max}\sqrt{\log d\cdot T/n}
and
\tau_{1}
and
\epsilon_{m}
are as defined in Lemma
4b
.
The estimator of Algorithm
1
after one-step update satisfies with probability at least
1-C^{\prime}_{3}/\{\log(nd)\}^{2}
,

\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\\
\leq\rho_{R}\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})+\frac{C^{\prime}_{2}}{1-\rho_{R}}\left\{\frac{1}{\omega_{\min}}\sqrt{T\frac{s_{1}\log d}{n}}+\max_{m}\sqrt{T\frac{(s_{2}+d_{m})\log d}{nd_{m}}}\right\},

where
C^{\prime}_{2},C^{\prime}_{3}>0
are constants and
\rho
given in (
A91
) satisfies
\rho\leq\rho_{R}\leq 1/2
with
\rho
given in Lemma
4b
.

Lemma 9b
.

Given
{\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)}
and
{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\omega}^{(t+1)}
satisfying that
\|{\bm{\beta}}_{m}^{(t)}\|_{2}=\|{\bm{\beta}}_{m}^{(t+1)}\|_{2}=1
, the update of ECM algorithm in Section
5.1
satisfies that

|\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|\leq\kappa|\omega^{(t)}|.

(A11)

If
\pi=1/2
,
\kappa=\gamma_{p}(\omega)=p+\frac{1-p}{1+\omega^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})}
with
p=\frac{1}{2}(1+\mathbb{P}_{Z\sim N(0,1)}(|Z|\leq 1))
. If
\pi\neq 1/2
,
\kappa=1-\frac{\rho^{2}}{2}
.

Lemma 10b
.

There exists positive constant
c
and
c^{\prime}
such that for any positive radius
r
, any
\delta\in(0,1)
, and any sample size
n\geq c^{\prime}\left(\sum_{m}d_{m}+\log(1/\delta)\right)
, the update in (
28
) satisfies that

\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{|\omega^{(t)}|\leq r}|\omega^{(t+1)}-\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|\leq c\sigma(\sigma r+\rho)\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{m}d_{m}+\log(1/\delta)}{n}}\right]\geq 1-\delta,

where
\rho=|1-2\pi|
.

Lemma 11b
.

The update of ECM algorithm in Section
5.2
satisfies that

|\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|\leq\left(1-\frac{2(\omega^{(t)})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)\omega^{(t)}.

(A12)

B2
Supporting lemmas

Lemma S1

(Lemma 2.7.7 of
Vershynin 2018
)
.

Let
X,Y
be two sub-Gaussian random variables. Then
Z=X\cdot Y
is sub-exponential random variable. Moreover, there exists a constant
C
such that

\|Z\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\|X\|_{\psi_{2}}\cdot\|Y\|_{\psi_{2}}.

Lemma S2

(Remark 5.18 of
Vershynin 2010
)
.

Let
X
be sub-Gaussian random variable and
Y
be sub-exponential random variables. Then
X-\mathbb{E}(X)
is also sub-Gaussian;
Y-\mathbb{E}(Y)
is also sub-exponential. Moreover, we have

\|X-\mathbb{E}(X)\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq 2\|X\|_{\psi_{2}},\|Y-\mathbb{E}(Y)\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq 2\|Y\|_{\psi_{1}}.

Lemma S3

(Theorem 2.6.2 of
Vershynin 2018
)
.

Suppose
X_{1},X_{2},\ldots,X_{n}
are i.i.d. centered sub-Gaussian random variables with
\|X_{1}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq K
. Then for every
t\geq 0
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\right\rvert\geq t\right)\leq e\cdot\exp(-\frac{Cnt^{2}}{K^{2}}),

where
C
is an absolute constant.

Lemma S4

(Corollary 2.8.3 of
Vershynin 2018
)
.

Suppose
X_{1},X_{2},\ldots,X_{n}
are i.i.d. centered sub-exponential random variables with
\|X_{1}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq K
. Then for every
t\geq 0
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\right\rvert\geq t\right)\leq 2\cdot\exp\left(-C\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{K^{2}},\frac{t}{K}\right\}n\right),

where
C
is an absolute constant.

Lemma S5

(Theorem 2.2.6 of
Vershynin 2018
)
.

Hoeffding’s inequality suppose
X_{1},X_{2},\ldots,X_{n}
are independent random variable,
a_{i}\leq X_{i}\leq b_{i}
, then we can have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}X_{i})\right\rvert\geq\epsilon\right)\leq 2\exp\left\{\frac{-2n^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(b_{i}-a_{i})^{2}}\right\}.

Moreover, if
a_{i}=0
and
b_{i}=1
, then we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}X_{i})\right\rvert\geq\epsilon\right)\leq 1-2e^{-2n\epsilon^{2}}.

Lemma S6

(Theorem 5.1.4 of
Vershynin 2018
)
.

Let
\bm{x}=\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, where
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
are i.i.d. with standard norm. Consider function
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with Lipschitz constant
L
, that is, for any vectors
\bm{v}_{1},\bm{v}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, there exists
L>0
such that
\left|f(\bm{v}_{1})-f(\bm{v}_{2})\right|\leq L\left\|\bm{v}_{1}-\bm{v}_{2}\right\|_{2}
. Then, for any
t>0
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|f(\bm{x})-\mathbb{E}\left(f(\bm{x})\right)\right|>t\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2L^{2}}\right).

Lemma S7
.

(Theorem 4.2.12 in
Horn et al. 1994
) Let
\lambda
be an eigenvalue of
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
with corresponding eigenvector
\bm{x}
, and let
\mu
be an eigenvalue of
\bm{B}^{n\times n}
with corresponding eigenvector
\bm{y}
. Then
\lambda\mu
is an eigenvalue of
\bm{A}\otimes\bm{B}
with a corresponding eigenvector given by
\bm{x}\otimes\bm{y}
. Any eigenvalue of
\bm{A}\otimes{\bf B}
arises as such a product of eigenvalues of
\bm{A}
and
\bm{B}
.

Lemma S8
.

(Lemma C.1. in
Cai et al. 2019
.)
Let
z^{(1)},\ldots,z^{(n)}
be n independent realizations of a random variable
z
and
\mathcal{F}
be a function class defined on the support of
z
. Suppose
\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{n}
are i.i.d. Rademacher random variables. Consider Lipschitz functions
\psi_{i}(\cdot)\,(i=1,\ldots,n)
with a Lipschitz constant
L
that satisfy
\psi(0)=0
. Then for any increasing convex function
\phi(\cdot)
and a fixed
g\in\mathcal{F}
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\phi\left(\left|\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}\cdot\psi_{i}[f(z^{(i)})]\cdot g(z^{(i)})\right|\right)\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\phi\left(2\left|L\cdot\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}\cdot f(z^{(i)})\cdot g(z^{(i)})\right|\right)\right].

Next, we state a number of supporting technical lemmas related to matrix and tensor algebra. Proofs of Lemmas
S9
-
S14
are delayed to Sections
E1
-
E6
, respectively.

Lemma S9
.

If a tensor
\bm{\mathcal{U}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}\times\cdots\times d_{M}}
admits the following decomposition

\bm{\mathcal{U}}=\omega\cdot{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\circ\cdots{\bm{\beta}}_{M},

then the mode-
m
matricization of
\bm{\mathcal{U}}
can be written as

\bm{\mathcal{U}}_{(m)}=\omega\cdot{\bm{\beta}}_{m}\mathrm{vec}\left({\bm{\beta}}_{1}\circ\cdots{\bm{\beta}}_{m-1}\circ{\bm{\beta}}_{m+1}\cdots{\bm{\beta}}_{M}\right)^{\top}.

Moreover, it holds that

\|\bm{\mathcal{U}}\|_{\text{F}}=\omega\|\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m\in[M]}{\bm{\beta}}_{m}\|_{\text{F}}=\omega.

Lemma S10
.

For
{\bm{Y}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{T}({\bf 0}_{d_{1}\times d_{2}};{\bf I}_{d_{1}},{\bf I}_{d_{2}})
and any matrix
{\bm{D}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}\times d_{2}}
, it holds that
\mathbb{E}({\bm{Y}}{\bm{D}}{\bm{Y}}^{\top})=\text{tr}({\bm{D}}){\bf I}_{d_{1}}
.

Lemma S11
.

Given unit vectors
\{{\bm{\beta}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\beta}}_{M}\}
and
\{{\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,{\bm{\beta}}_{M}^{\prime}\}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m}{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m})^{\top}\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m}{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime})\geq\prod_{m}(1-\|{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime}\|_{2}),

\Big\|\prod_{m}^{\circ}{\bm{\beta}}_{m}-\prod_{m}^{\circ}{\bm{\beta}}_{m}^{\prime}\Big\|_{\text{F}}\leq\sum_{m}\left\|{\bm{\beta}}_{m}-{\bm{\beta}}_{m}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}

\|\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m}{\bm{\beta}}_{m})-\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m}{\bm{\beta}}_{m}^{\prime})\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{M}\sum_{m}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{m}-{\bm{\beta}}_{m}^{\prime}\|_{2}.

Lemma S12
.

Let
{\bm{\Omega}}_{m}\,{\bm{\Omega}}_{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{m}\times d_{m}}
satisfies that
\|{\bm{\Omega}}_{m}\|_{\text{F}}=\|{\bm{\Omega}}_{m}^{\prime}\|_{\text{F}}=\sqrt{d_{m}}
. It holds that

\Big\|\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m}{\bm{\Omega}}_{m}-\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m}{\bm{\Omega}}_{m}^{\prime}\Big\|_{\text{F}}\leq\sum_{m}\sqrt{\frac{d}{d_{m}}}\|{\bm{\Omega}}_{m}-{\bm{\Omega}}_{m}^{\prime}\|_{\text{F}}.

Lemma S13
.

For any
{\bm{\beta}}_{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{m}}
with
\|{\bm{\beta}}_{m}\|_{2}=1
, we can construct orthonormal matrices
\bm{R}_{m}
such that
\bm{R}_{m}{\bm{\beta}}_{m}=e_{1}(d_{m})
, where
e_{1}(d_{m})\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{m}}
is a vector with the first element as
1
and all other elements as
0
. For a tensor
\bm{\mathcal{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}\times d_{3}}
, we have

\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\mathcal{X}})^{\top}\mathrm{vec}({\bm{\beta}}_{1}\circ{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\circ{\bm{\beta}}_{3})=\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{1,1,1},

where
\bm{\mathcal{V}}=\bm{\mathcal{X}}\times_{1}\bm{R}_{1}\times_{2}\bm{R}_{2}\times_{3}\bm{R}_{3}
.

Lemma S14
.

The first- and second- partial derivatives of
Q_{n}
in (
7
) with respect to
{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}
,
\omega_{k}
and
{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}
are given as below. Here,

\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{\bm{\vartheta}}
denotes the parameter being conditioned on, with
\bm{\vartheta}
removed. Note that the dependence of
Q_{n}(\bm{\vartheta}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-\bm{\vartheta}}|{\bm{\Theta}})
on
{\bm{\Theta}}
only comes via
\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})
.

\displaystyle\nabla_{{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}Q_{n}({\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})

(A13)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})\bar{{\bm{\Omega}}}_{k,m}\left\{(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i})_{(m)}-\bar{\omega}_{k}{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime}\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m^{\prime}\neq m}\bar{{\bm{\beta}}}_{k,m^{\prime}})\right\}\left(\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m^{\prime}\neq m}\bar{{\bm{\Omega}}}_{k,m^{\prime}}\right)\bar{\omega}_{k}\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m^{\prime}\neq m}\bar{{\bm{\beta}}}_{k,m^{\prime}});

\displaystyle\nabla_{\omega_{k}}Q_{n}(\omega_{k}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-\omega_{k}}|{\bm{\Theta}})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})\{\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i})-\omega_{k}^{\prime}\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m}\bar{{\bm{\beta}}}_{k,m})\}^{\top}\left(\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m}\bar{{\bm{\Omega}}}_{k,m}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m}\bar{{\bm{\beta}}}_{k,m});

\displaystyle\nabla_{{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}Q_{n}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})\left\{\frac{d}{2d_{m}}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime})^{-1}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\bar{\bm{\mathcal{U}}}_{k}\right)_{(m)}\left(\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m^{\prime}\neq m}\bar{{\bm{\Omega}}}_{k,m^{\prime}}\right)\left(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\bar{\bm{\mathcal{U}}}_{k}\right)_{(m)}^{\top}\right\}

\displaystyle\nabla_{{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}^{2}Q_{n}({\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\beta}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})\bar{\omega}_{k}^{2}\left\{\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m^{\prime}\neq m}\bar{{\bm{\beta}}}_{k,m^{\prime}})^{\top}\left(\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m^{\prime}\neq m}\bar{{\bm{\Omega}}}_{k,m^{\prime}}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m^{\prime}\neq m}\bar{{\bm{\beta}}}_{k,m^{\prime}})\right\}\bar{{\bm{\Omega}}}_{k,m};

\displaystyle\nabla_{\omega_{k}}^{2}Q_{n}(\omega_{k}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-\omega_{k}}|{\bm{\Theta}})=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m}\bar{{\bm{\beta}}}_{k,m})^{\top}\left(\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m}\bar{{\bm{\Omega}}}_{k,m}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m}\bar{{\bm{\beta}}}_{k,m});

\displaystyle\nabla_{{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}^{2}Q_{n}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime},\bar{{\bm{\Theta}}}_{-{\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}}|{\bm{\Theta}})=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})\left\{\frac{d}{2d_{m}}({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime})^{-1}\otimes({\bm{\Omega}}_{k,m}^{\prime})^{-1}\right\}.

For
\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})
in (
6
),
\nabla_{{\bm{\Theta}}}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})
is expressed as

\left(\left[\nabla_{{{\bm{\theta}}_{1}}}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})\right]^{\top},\cdots,\left[\nabla_{{{\bm{\theta}}_{K}}}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})\right]^{\top}\right)^{\top}
,
where

\nabla_{{{\bm{\theta}}_{l}}}\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})\tau_{il}({\bm{\Theta}})J_{i}({\bm{\theta}}_{l}),&\text{when }l\neq k,\\
\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}})(1-\tau_{ik}({\bm{\Theta}}))J_{i}({\bm{\theta}}_{l}),&\text{when }l=k.\end{array}\right.

We write
J_{i}({\bm{\theta}}_{l})=(J_{i,1}({\bm{\theta}}_{l}),J_{i,2}({\bm{\theta}}_{l}),J_{i,3}({\bm{\theta}}_{l}))
, where
J_{i,1}({\bm{\theta}}_{l})
are from all
{\bm{\beta}}_{l,m}
,
J_{i,2}({\bm{\theta}}_{l})
are from
\omega_{l}
and
J_{i,3}({\bm{\theta}}_{l})
are from all
{\bm{\Omega}}_{l,m}
. The expression of
J_{i,j}({\bm{\theta}}_{l})
are

\displaystyle J_{i,1}({\bm{\theta}}_{l})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{{\bm{\Omega}}_{l,m}\left(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\bm{\mathcal{U}}_{l}\right)_{(m)}\left(\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m^{\prime}\neq m}{\bm{\Omega}}_{l,m^{\prime}}\right)\omega_{l}\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m^{\prime}\neq m}{\bm{\beta}}_{l,m^{\prime}})\right\}_{m\in[M]},

(A14)

\displaystyle J_{i,2}({\bm{\theta}}_{l})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\bm{\mathcal{U}}_{l})^{\top}\left(\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m}{\bm{\Omega}}_{l,m}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\prod\limits^{\circ}_{m^{\prime}}{\bm{\beta}}_{l,m^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle J_{i,3}({\bm{\theta}}_{l})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{vec}\left\{\frac{d}{2d_{m}}{\bm{\Omega}}_{l,m}^{-1}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\bm{\mathcal{U}}_{l}\right)_{(m)}\left(\prod\limits^{\otimes}_{m^{\prime}\neq m}{\bm{\Omega}}_{l,m^{\prime}}\right)\left(\bm{\mathcal{X}}_{i}-\bm{\mathcal{U}}_{l}\right)_{(m)}^{\top}\right\}_{m\in[M]}.

C
Proof of Main Results

C1
Proof of Proposition
1

Balakrishnan et al. 2017
considers
\bm{X}\sim\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\mathcal{N}({\bm{\mu}}^{\ast},\sigma^{2}\bm{1}_{d})}_{\text{cluster 1}}+\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\mathcal{N}(-{\bm{\mu}}^{\ast},\sigma^{2}\bm{1}_{d})}_{\text{cluster 2}}
and
{\bm{\mu}}\in\{{\bm{\mu}}\,|\,\|{\bm{\mu}}-{\bm{\mu}}^{\ast}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}{\|{\bm{\mu}}^{\ast}\|_{2}}\}
. They assumed
\sigma^{2}
is known, and thus, without loss of generality, we let
\sigma^{2}=1
in this proof.
Under this model, the parameter vector
{\bm{\Theta}}
reduces to
{\bm{\mu}}
. Note that in this case of
M=1
, we do not need to normalize
{\bm{\mu}}
as there is no identifiability issue.

Suppose
k=2
and
l=1
. For
{\bm{\mu}},{\bm{\mu}}^{\prime}\in\{{\bm{\mu}}|\frac{\|{\bm{\mu}}-{\bm{\mu}}^{\ast}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\mu}}^{\ast}\|_{2}}\leq\frac{1}{4}\}
, we have

W_{ikl}^{2}=\left\|\bm{X}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\bm{X}_{i}+{\bm{\mu}}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

By the definition of
\tau_{ik}({\bm{\mu}})
, we have

\tau_{i1}({\bm{\mu}})\tau_{i2}({\bm{\mu}})=\frac{1}{\{\exp(-\eta_{1}({\bm{\mu}})+\eta_{2}({\bm{\mu}}))+\exp(\eta_{1}({\bm{\mu}})-\eta_{2}({\bm{\mu}}))\}^{2}}

with
\eta_{1}({\bm{\mu}})=\frac{1}{4}\left\|\bm{X}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}\right\|_{2}^{2}
and
\eta_{2}({\bm{\mu}})=\frac{1}{4}\left\|\bm{X}_{i}+{\bm{\mu}}\right\|_{2}^{2}
.

Define
\bm{A}_{1}=\left\{\bm{X}_{i}:(1-c)\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})\geq\eta_{2}(\bm{\mu})\right\}
,
\bm{A}_{2}=\left\{\bm{X}_{i}:(1-c)\eta_{2}(\bm{\mu})\geq\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})\right\}
and
\bm{A}_{3}=(\bm{A}_{1}\cup\bm{A}_{2})^{c}
, where
\bm{A}^{c}
is the complement of
\bm{A}
and
c\in(0,1)
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{W_{i21}\tau_{i1}(\bm{\mu})\tau_{i2}(\bm{\mu})\right\}^{2}=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{W_{i21}\tau_{i1}(\bm{\mu})\tau_{i2}(\bm{\mu})\right\}^{2}|\bm{A}_{1}\right]\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{1})}_{(i)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{W_{i21}\tau_{i1}(\bm{\mu})\tau_{i2}(\bm{\mu})\right\}^{2}|\bm{A}_{2}\right]\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{2})}_{(ii)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{W_{i21}\tau_{i1}(\bm{\mu})\tau_{i2}(\bm{\mu})\right\}^{2}|\bm{A}_{3}\right]\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{3})}_{(iii)}.

Let
\alpha_{0}=\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}/16
and
c=1/2
. In what follows, we discuss the upper bounds of terms
(i)
,
(ii)
and
(iii)
respectively.

Part
(i)
.

Conditioning on
\bm{A}_{1}
, it is seen that
\tau_{i1}(\bm{\mu})\tau_{i2}(\bm{\mu})\leq\exp(-2c\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu}))
.
Moreover, by noting
\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})>\eta_{2}(\bm{\mu})
under
\bm{A}_{1}
and
\left\|\bm{\mu}^{\prime}-\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 4\alpha_{0}
,
it holds that

4\eta_{2}(\bm{\mu}^{\prime})=\left\|\bm{X}_{i}+\bm{\mu}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\left\|\bm{X}_{i}+\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\left\|\bm{\mu}^{\prime}-\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 8\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})+8\alpha_{0}.

Correspondingly, assuming
\|{\bf u}^{\ast}\|_{2}
is sufficiently large (e.g.
\|{\bf u}^{\ast}\|_{2}\geq 8/3
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{W_{i21}\tau_{i1}(\bm{\mu})\tau_{i2}(\bm{\mu})\right\}^{2}|\bm{A}_{1}\right]\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{1})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})\eta_{2}(\bm{\mu}^{\prime})}{\exp(2\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu}))}|\bm{A}_{1}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\eta_{1}^{2}(\bm{\mu})}{\exp(2\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu}))}|\bm{A}_{1}\right\}+\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\alpha_{0}\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})}{\exp(2\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu}))}|\bm{A}_{1}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{4}+4\alpha_{0}\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{16}\exp(-\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}/2),

where the last inequality is due to
\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})\geq\frac{9}{64}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}
as
4\|\bm{\mu}-\bm{X}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\geq 2\|\bm{\mu}-\bm{X}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}+2\|\bm{X}_{i}+\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\|2\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}
under
\bm{A}_{1}
and
\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\geq\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}-\|\bm{\mu}-\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}\geq\frac{3}{4}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}
, and
\sup\limits_{t\geq t^{\ast}}{t}{\exp(-at)}={t^{\ast}}{\exp(-at^{\ast})}
,
\sup\limits_{t\geq t^{\ast}}{t^{2}}{\exp(-at)}={(t^{\ast})^{2}}{\exp(-at^{\ast})}
when
t^{\ast}\geq 2/a
.

Part
(ii)
.

Using a similar argument as in Part
(i)
, we can get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{W_{i21}\tau_{i1}(\bm{\mu})\tau_{i2}(\bm{\mu})\right\}^{2}|\bm{A}_{2}\right]\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{2})\leq\frac{\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{4}+4\alpha_{0}\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{16}\exp(-\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}/2).

Part
(iii)
.

Define
\bm{B}_{j}=\{\bm{X}_{i}\mid(j-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}<4\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})\leq j\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\}
for
j=1,2,\ldots
. It then holds that
\bm{A}_{3}=\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j}
. Conditioning on
\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j}
, it is seen that
\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})\leq{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}/4
and

4\eta_{2}(\bm{\mu}^{\prime})=\left\|\bm{X}_{i}+\bm{\mu}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\left\|\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\left\|\bm{\mu}+\bm{\mu}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq(8j+10)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2},

where the last inequality holds due to
\left\|\bm{\mu}^{\prime}+\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\left\|\bm{\mu}+\bm{\mu}^{\prime}-2\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\left\|2\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 10\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}
.
As
\tau_{i1}(\bm{\mu})\tau_{i2}(\bm{\mu})\leq\frac{1}{4}
, we can write

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{W_{i21}\tau_{i1}(\bm{\mu})\tau_{i2}(\bm{\mu})\right\}^{2}|\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j}\right]\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j})\leq\frac{j(8j+10)}{64}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{4}\times\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j}).

(A15)

Next, we bound
\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j})
for a given
j
. By the definition of
\bm{A}_{1}
and
\bm{A}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\bm{A}_{3}

\displaystyle=\left\{\bm{X}:\eta_{2}(\bm{\mu})/2\leq\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})\leq 2\eta_{2}(\bm{\mu})\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\bm{X}:\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})/2\leq\eta_{2}(\bm{\mu})\leq 2\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})\right\}.

We also have
\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})+\eta_{2}(\bm{\mu})\geq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}
, as
2\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\bm{\mu}-\bm{X}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{X}_{i}+\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}
. With
\eta_{2}(\bm{\mu})\leq 2\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})
, we obtain that
\left\|\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{2}{3}\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}
. Similarly,
\left\|\bm{X}_{i}+\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{2}{3}\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}
also holds.
Correspondingly, conditioning on
\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j}
, we have

\displaystyle 4\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})=\|\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}:

\displaystyle j\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\geq 4\eta_{1}(\bm{\mu})\geq\max\{2\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}/3,(j-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\};

\displaystyle 4\eta_{2}(\bm{\mu})=\|\bm{X}_{i}+\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}:

\displaystyle 2j\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\geq 4\eta_{2}(\bm{\mu})\geq\max\{2\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}/3,(j-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}/2\}.

Letting
Z_{i}
denote the latent cluster label of
\bm{X}_{i}
, we can then write
\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j})=\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j}\mid Z_{i}=1)P(Z_{i}=1)+\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j}|Z_{i}=2)P(Z_{i}=2)
.

For
\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j}\mid Z_{i}=1)
, it can be bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j}\mid Z_{i}=1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}\geq\left\|\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}\geq a_{j}\mid Z_{i}=1\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}+\frac{1}{4}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}\geq\left\|\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\right\|_{2}\geq a_{j}-\frac{1}{4}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}\mid Z_{i}=1\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left((\sqrt{j}+\frac{1}{4})^{2}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\left\|\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq(\sqrt{j-1}-\frac{1}{4})^{2}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\mid Z_{i}=1\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2})2^{d/2}}\int_{l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t

where
a_{j}=\max\left\{\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2},\sqrt{j-1}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}\right\}
,
l_{j}=(\sqrt{j-1}-\frac{1}{4})^{2}
and
u_{j}=(\sqrt{j}+\frac{1}{4})^{2}
and the second inequality holds due to
\|\bm{\mu}-\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}
.
We claim that, for any
j
, it holds for some
a_{j}\in[l_{j},u_{j}]
that

\int_{l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t\leq 4j\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\int_{a_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{(a_{j}+1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t.

This claim can be shown by considering three scenarios by noting
\int t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t
is proportional to the pdf of
\chi^{2}_{d}
.
As the mode of
\int t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t
is
d-2
, the function is increasing in
(0,d-2]
and decreasing in
[d-2,\infty)
.
We consider: (a)
u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\leq d-2
, (b)
l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\geq d-2
and (c)
l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}<d-2<u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}
.

Case (a).

In this case, noting
l_{j}<u_{j}-1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t=

\displaystyle\int_{l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{(u_{j}-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t+\int_{(u_{j}-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t.

(A16)

Since
t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}
is an increasing function in
[l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2},u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}]
, we can get that

\displaystyle\int_{l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{(u_{j}-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t\leq(u_{j}-l_{j}-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\{(u_{j}-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\}^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-(u_{j}-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}/2};

\displaystyle\int_{(u_{j}-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t\geq\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\{(u_{j}-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\}^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-(u_{j}-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}/2}.

Combining the above results together, it then follows that

\int_{l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{(u_{j}-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t\leq(u_{j}-l_{j}-1)\int_{(u_{j}-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t.

Plugging this into (
A16
) and we have

\int_{l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t\leq(u_{j}-l_{j})\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\int_{(u_{j}-1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t.

Letting
a_{j}=u_{j}-1
and by noting
(u_{j}-l_{j})/j\leq 4
, our claim can be verified under case (a). Case (b) can be verified similarly and we omit the detailed derivations here.

Case (c).

We further consider under this case two scenarios, namely, (c.1)
d+2-l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}
and
u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}-d-2\geq\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}
and (c.2)
d+2-l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}
or
u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}-d-2\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}
. Under (c.1), following a similar argument as in Case (a), we have

\displaystyle\int_{l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t=\int_{l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{d+2}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t+\int_{d+2}^{u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t

(A17)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2(d+2-l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2})\int_{d+2-\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{d+2}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t+2(u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}-d-2)\int_{d+2}^{d+2+\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2(u_{j}-l_{j})\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\int_{d+2-\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{d+2+\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t.

Letting
a_{j}=d+2-\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}
and by noting
2(u_{j}-l_{j})/j\leq 4
, our claim can be verified under case (c.1).
Under (c.2), show the claim for
d+2-l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}
. The case of
u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}-d-2\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}
follows a similar argument.
We have

\displaystyle\int_{l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t=\int_{l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{d+2}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t+\int_{d+2}^{u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t

(A18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{d+2}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t+2(u_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}-d-2)\int_{d+2}^{d+2+\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2(u_{j}-l_{j})\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\int_{l_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{(l_{j}+1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t.

Letting
a_{j}=l_{j}
and by noting
2(u_{j}-l_{j})/j\leq 4
, our claim can be verified under case (c.2).

Putting together cases (a), (b) and (c), we have

\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j}\mid Z_{i}=1)\leq\frac{4j\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2})2^{d/2}}\int_{a_{j}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{(a_{j}+1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t

Using a similar argument, we can also show that

\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j}|Z_{i}=2)\leq\frac{8j\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2})2^{d/2}}\int_{a_{j}^{\prime}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{(a_{j}^{\prime}+1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t,

where
a_{j}^{\prime}\in[(\sqrt{(j-1)/2}-1/4)^{2},(\sqrt{j/2}+1/4)^{2}]
.
As
\mathbb{P}(Z_{i}=1)=\mathbb{P}(Z_{i}=2)=\frac{1}{2}
, we can conclude that

\mathbb{P}(\bm{A}_{3}\cap\bm{B}_{j})\leq\frac{8j\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2})2^{d/2}}\int_{\min\{a_{j},a_{j}^{\prime}\}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{(\max\{a_{j},a_{j}^{\prime}\}+1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t.

Plugging this result into (
A15
), we have

\displaystyle(iii)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{8j^{2}(8j+10)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{6}}{64\Gamma(\frac{d}{2})2^{d/2}}\int_{\min\{a_{j},a_{j}^{\prime}\}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{(\max\{a_{j},a_{j}^{\prime}\}+1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{c_{4}(d/2+2)(d/2+1)d/2}{\Gamma(\frac{d+6}{2})2^{(d+6)/2}}\int_{\min\{a_{j},a_{j}^{\prime}\}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{(\max\{a_{j},a_{j}^{\prime}\}+1)\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}t^{\frac{d+6}{2}-1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{c_{5}}{\Gamma(\frac{d+6}{2})2^{(d+6)/2}}\int_{\frac{1}{16}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}}^{\infty}t^{\frac{d+6}{2}+1}e^{-t/2}\text{d}t=c_{5}\mathbb{P}(\chi^{2}_{d+6}>\frac{1}{16}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}),

where
c_{5}=4c_{4}d(d/2+1)(d/2+2)
and the second inequality uses the facts that
\Gamma(\frac{d+6}{2})=\frac{d}{2}(\frac{d}{2}+1)(\frac{d}{2}+2)\Gamma(\frac{d}{2})
and there must exists a positive constant
c_{4}
such that
j^{2}(8j+10)\leq 8c_{4}(t^{\prime}/2)^{3}
for any
\min\{a_{j},a_{j}^{\prime}\}\leq t^{\prime}\leq\max\{a_{j},a_{j}^{\prime}\}+1
and the last inequality holds as
\min_{j}\{a_{j},a_{j}^{\prime}\}\geq\frac{1}{16}
.

Combining Steps
(i)
,
(ii)
and
(iii)
, it holds that

\mathbb{E}\left\{W_{i21}\tau_{i1}(\bm{\mu})\tau_{i2}(\bm{\mu})\right\}^{2}\leq\frac{\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{4}+4\alpha_{0}\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{8}\exp(-\left\|\bm{\mu}\right\|_{2}^{2}/2)+c_{5}P(\chi^{2}_{d+6}>\frac{1}{16}\|\bm{\mu}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}),

and we arrive at the desired result by noting
\|{\bm{\mu}}-{\bm{\mu}}^{\ast}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}{\|{\bm{\mu}}^{\ast}\|_{2}}
.

C2
Proof of Theorem
1

We consider the induction method for this proof. At
t=1
, givens Condition
1
-
4
, Lemma
4b
ensures that it holds with probability at least
1-{C_{3}}/\{\log(nd)\}^{2}
,

\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\leq\epsilon+\rho\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}),

where
\rho
is as defined in Lemma
4b
and

\epsilon=C_{2}\left\{\frac{1}{\omega_{\min}}\sqrt{T\frac{s_{1}\log d}{n}}+\max_{m}\sqrt{\frac{(s_{2}+d_{m})\log d\cdot T}{nd_{m}}}\right\},

where
C_{2}
is as defined in Lemma
4b
.
At step
t>1
, suppose it holds with probability at least
1-{C_{3}t}/\{\log(nd)\}^{2}
that

\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\leq\frac{1-\rho^{t}}{1-\rho}\epsilon+\rho^{t}\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}).

Then using the same argument as in Step 2 of the proof for Lemma
4b
, it holds that
{\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)}
satisfies Condition
2
. Applying Lemma
4b
for
\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t+1)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
, it follows that

\displaystyle\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t+1)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon+\rho\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon+\rho\left\{\frac{1-\rho^{t}}{1-\rho}\epsilon+\rho^{t}\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\rho^{t+1}}{1-\rho}\epsilon+\rho^{t+1}\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}).

holds with probability at least
1-{C_{3}(t+1)}/\{\log(nd)\}^{2}
. As such, the contraction inequality also holds for step
t+1
.

It is then seen that
\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t+1)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\leq\frac{1}{1-\rho}\epsilon+\rho^{t+1}\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
for
t=1,\ldots,T
.
Since
\rho\in(0,1/3]
, the term
\frac{1}{1-\rho}\epsilon
will dominate when it reaches
T=\log(\frac{\epsilon}{(1-\rho)\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})})/\log\rho
steps.
From
\log d\asymp\log d_{\max}
and
\omega_{\min}\precsim d^{M/2}
by Condition
1
, it then holds that
\log(1/\epsilon)\precsim\log(nd_{\max})
.
Therefore,
T\precsim(-\log\rho)^{-1}\log(d_{\max}n\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}))
.
For
t\leq T
, the probability for the contraction inequality to hold can be calculated as

\frac{C_{3}t}{\{\log(nd)\}^{2}}\precsim C_{3}\frac{\log(d_{\max}n\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}))}{\log(\rho)\{\log(nd)\}^{2}}=o(1).

Putting the above results together, we arrive at that, for
t\leq T
,

\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t+1)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\leq\frac{1}{1-\rho}\epsilon+\rho^{t+1}\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}),

holds with probability
1-o(1)
.

C3
Proof of Theorem
2

We consider the induction method for this proof. At
t=1
, givens Condition
1
-
4
, Lemma
8b
ensures that it holds with probability at least
1-{C^{\prime}_{3}}/\{\log(nd)\}^{2}
,

\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\leq\epsilon^{\prime}+\rho_{R}\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}),

where
\rho_{R}
is as defined in Lemma
8b
and

\epsilon^{\prime}=C^{\prime}_{2}\left\{\frac{1}{\omega_{\min}}\sqrt{T\frac{s_{1}\log d}{n}}+\max_{m}\sqrt{T\frac{(s_{2}+d_{m})\log d}{nd_{m}}}\right\},

where
C^{\prime}_{2}
is as defined in Lemma
8b
.
At step
t>1
, suppose it holds with probability at least
1-{C^{\prime}_{3}t}/\{\log(nd)\}^{2}
that

\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\leq\frac{1-\rho_{R}^{t}}{1-\rho_{R}}\epsilon^{\prime}+\rho_{R}^{t}\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}).

Then using the same argument as in Step 2 of the proof for Lemma
8b
, it holds that
{\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)}
satisfies Condition
2
. Applying Lemma
8b
for
\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t+1)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
, it follows that

\displaystyle\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t+1)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{\prime}+\rho_{R}\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{\prime}+\rho_{R}\left\{\frac{1-\rho_{R}^{t}}{1-\rho_{R}}\epsilon^{\prime}+\rho_{R}^{t}\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-\rho_{R}^{t+1}}{1-\rho_{R}}\epsilon^{\prime}+\rho_{R}^{t+1}\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}).

holds with probability at least
1-{C^{\prime}_{3}(t+1)}/\{\log(nd)\}^{2}
. As such, the contraction inequality also holds for step
t+1
.

It is then seen that
\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t+1)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\leq\frac{1}{1-\rho_{R}}\epsilon^{\prime}+\rho_{R}^{t+1}\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})
for
t=1,\ldots,T
.
Since
\rho_{R}\in(0,1/2]
, the term
\frac{1}{1-\rho_{R}}\epsilon^{\prime}
will dominate when it reaches
T=\log(\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{(1-\rho_{R})\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})})/\log\rho_{R}
steps.
Since
\log d\asymp\log d_{\max}
and
\omega_{\min}\precsim d^{M/2}
by Condition
1
, it then holds that
\log(1/\epsilon^{\prime})\precsim\log(nd_{\max})
.
Therefore,
T\precsim(-\log\rho_{R})^{-1}\log(d_{\max}n\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}))
.
For
t\leq T
, the probability for the contraction inequality to hold can be calculated as

\frac{C^{\prime}_{3}t}{\{\log(nd)\}^{2}}\precsim C^{\prime}_{3}\frac{\log(d_{\max}n\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}))}{\log(\rho)\{\log(nd)\}^{2}}=o(1).

Putting the above results together, we arrive at that, for
t\leq T
,

\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(t+1)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast})\leq\frac{1}{1-\rho_{R}}\epsilon^{\prime}+\rho_{R}^{t+1}\textrm{D}({\bm{\Theta}}^{(0)},{\bm{\Theta}}^{\ast}),

holds with probability
1-o(1)
.

C4
Proof of Theorem
3

Under the setting in Section
5.1
, the data are generated from a tensor normal distribution with a mean of
\mathbf{0}
. In this case
\omega^{\ast}=0
, and the vectors
{\bm{\beta}}_{m}^{\ast}
can be any unit vectors. Hence, our analysis focuses on the estimation error of
\omega^{*}
. Our proof is based on the following inequality:

|\omega^{(t+1)}|\leq|\omega^{(t+1)}-\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|+|\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|.

(A19)

Given this, we aim to bound
|\omega^{(t+1)}-\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|
and
|\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|
individually.
The error term
|\omega^{(t+1)}-\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|
is bounded using Lemma
10b
, a key lemma in this analysis.
The proof of Lemma
10b
presents two challenges. First, we need to consider the low-rank structure of the mean, which simplifies the parameter space and reduces the statistical error from
O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\prod_{m}d_{m}}{n}}\right)
to
O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{m}d_{m}}{n}}\right)
. This requires a tighter bound compared to
Dwivedi et al. 2020
. Second, the update of
\omega
follows the form in (
28
), which involves a conditional maximization problem with newly updated conditional parameters
{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\omega}^{(t+1)}
. Note that
{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\omega}^{(t+1)}
and
{\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)}
both depend on data
\underline{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}
. As a result, the statistical error for
|\omega^{(t+1)}-\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|
needs to be established uniformly for all possible
{\bm{\Theta}}^{(t)}
and
{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\omega}^{(t+1)}
. Regarding
|\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|
, it can be bounded by Lemma
9b
. Compared to the vectorized model setting in
Dwivedi et al. 2020
, the proof of Lemma
9b
is more challenging due to two main aspects. Firstly, some techniques cannot be directly applied because of the low-rank decomposition of the tensor means. Secondly, the newly updated parameters
{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\omega}^{(t+1)}
are related to data
\underline{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}
, and hence we need to show that Lemma
9b
holds uniformly for all possible
{\bm{\Theta}}_{-\omega}^{(t+1)}
. Next, we outline the proof strategies.

To establish (
29
), we employ the strategy of annulus-based localization of epochs introduced in
Dwivedi et al. 2020
. In this strategy, we define a sequence of outer radius for each annulus as follows:

\mathcal{R}=\left\{\omega^{(0)},\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{0}},\ldots,\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l_{\alpha}-1}}\right\},

(A20)

where
\zeta=\sigma^{2}\frac{\sum_{m}d_{m}+\log((2l_{\alpha}+1)/\delta)}{n}
,
\alpha_{l}
is a decreasing positive sequence and
l_{\alpha}
is the integer satisfying that
\alpha_{l_{\alpha}-1}\geq\frac{1}{4}-\alpha
. The specific forms of
\alpha_{l}
and
l_{\alpha}
will be decided later in this proof.
The entire sequence of sample ECM iterations is divided into a sequence of epochs. In each epoch, the ECM iterates are localized in to an annulus. That is, the
l
th epoch is defined to be the set of ECM iterations such that the iterate falls in the
l
th annulus.
Specifically, the proof can be summarized into four steps. In Step 1, we show that given
|\omega^{(t)}|\leq\sqrt{2}\sigma\omega^{\alpha_{l}}
, if the sample size is large enough,
|\omega^{(t_{0})}|\leq\sqrt{2}\sigma\omega^{\alpha_{l}}
for any
t_{0}>t
. This can guarantee the updated parameter is either in this annulus or a smaller adjacent one. In Step 2, we use the rate of statistical error and contraction rate to set the outer bound of each annulus. In Step 3, we show that the updated parameter will go into the next smaller annulus until
|\omega^{(t)}|=O\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)^{1/4-\alpha}
for any
\alpha\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
. In Step 4, the final result in (
29
) can be obtained by putting everything together. In the following proof, we will provide detailed explanations for each of these four steps.

Step 1:
In this step, we show the non-expansive property of our algorithm. This means given
|\omega^{(t)}|\leq\sqrt{2}\sigma\omega^{\alpha_{l}}
, if the sample size is large enough, we have
|\omega^{(t_{0})}|\leq\sqrt{2}\sigma\omega^{\alpha_{l}}
for any
t_{0}>t
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
|\omega^{(t)}|\in[\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l+1}},\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l+1}})
.

By Lemma
9b
and Lemma
10b
, we have

\displaystyle|\omega^{(t+1)}|

\displaystyle\leq|\omega^{(t+1)}-\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|+|\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|

(A21)

\displaystyle\leq c\sigma r\sqrt{\zeta}+\gamma_{p}(\omega^{(t)})|\omega^{(t)}|

\displaystyle\leq c\sigma\cdot\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l}}\sqrt{\zeta}+(1-\frac{1-p}{2}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}})\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{1-p}{2}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}+c\sigma\sqrt{\zeta}\right)\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l}},

with probability at least
1-\delta
. The second inequality is the direct result of Lemma
9b
and Lemma
10b
. The third one follows that
|\omega^{(t)}|\leq\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l}}
and

\gamma_{p}(\omega^{(t)})=p+\frac{1-p}{1+\frac{(\omega^{(t)})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\leq p+\frac{1-p}{1+\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}}=1-(1-p)\frac{\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}}{1+\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}}\leq 1-\frac{1-p}{2}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}.

(A22)

This is true, because
|\omega^{(t)}|\geq\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l+1}}
and
\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}\leq 1
when
n
is sufficiently large. Note that
\alpha_{l+1}\geq\frac{1}{4}-\alpha
for all
l\leq l_{\alpha}-1
and
\omega\leq 1
. As a result, for
n\geq\sigma^{2}(2c\sigma/(1-p))^{1/(2\epsilon)}(\sum_{m}d_{m}+\log((2l_{\alpha}+1)/\delta))
,

-\frac{1-p}{2}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}+c\sigma\sqrt{\zeta}\leq-c\sigma\sqrt{\zeta}\left\{\frac{1-p}{2c\sigma}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}-\frac{1}{2}}-1\right\}\leq-c\sigma\sqrt{\zeta}\left\{\frac{1-p}{2c\sigma}\zeta^{-2\alpha}-1\right\}\leq 0.

It is then straightforward to get that

1-\frac{1-p}{2}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}+c\sigma\sqrt{\zeta}\leq 1.

Repeating (
A21
) for multiple times, we can get that

|\omega^{(t_{0})}|\leq\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l}}

for any
t_{0}\geq t
.

Step 2:
In this step, we move to decide the outer bound of each annulus. This means we should specify
\alpha_{l}
in this part. Given
|\omega^{(t)}|\in[\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l+1}},\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l}}]
, we can use Lemma
9b
to get that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|\leq\left(p+\frac{1-p}{1+\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}}\right)|\omega^{(t)}|

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{(1-p)\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}}{1+\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}}\right)|\omega^{(t)}|

(A23)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{1-p}{2}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}\right)|\omega^{(t)}|\leq e^{-\frac{1-p}{2}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}}|\omega^{(t)}|.

This is true, because
e^{-\frac{1-p}{2}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}}=1-\frac{1-p}{2}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1-p}{2}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}\right)^{2}+o\left(\frac{(1-p)^{2}}{4}\zeta^{4\alpha_{l+1}}\right)
. On the other hand, using the upper bound
\omega^{(t)}
and Lemma
10b
, there exist positive constant
c_{1}
such that

|\omega^{(t)}-\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t)}]|\leq c\sigma^{2}r\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{m}d_{m}+\log(1/\delta)}{n}}\leq c_{1}\left(\frac{\sum_{m}d_{m}}{n}\right)^{\alpha_{l}+\frac{1}{2}},

(A24)

with probability at least
1-\delta
. Letting
\tilde{\gamma}=e^{-\frac{1-p}{2}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}}
, after
t_{l}
steps, we have

\displaystyle|\omega^{(t+t_{l})}|

\displaystyle\leq|\omega^{(t)}-\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t)}]|(1+\tilde{\gamma}+\cdots+\tilde{\gamma}^{t_{l}-1})+\tilde{\gamma}^{t_{l}}|\omega^{(t)}|

(A25)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\omega^{(t)}-\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t)}]|}{1-\tilde{\gamma}}+e^{-\frac{1-p}{2}t_{l}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}}\cdot\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l}}.

The second term decays exponentially in
t_{l}
and it will be dominated by the first term when
t_{l}
is sufficiently large. This means

|\omega^{(t+t_{l})}|\preceq\frac{|\omega^{(t)}-\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t)}]|}{1-\tilde{\gamma}}\approx\left(\frac{\sum_{m}d_{m}}{n}\right)^{-2\alpha_{l+1}}\left(\frac{\sum_{m}d_{m}}{n}\right)^{\alpha_{l}+\frac{1}{2}}.

This is true, because
\tilde{\gamma}=1-\frac{1-p}{2}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1-p}{2}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}\right)^{2}+o\left(\frac{(1-p)^{2}}{4}\zeta^{4\alpha_{l+1}}\right)
. Note that the epoch is said to be complete once
|\zeta^{(t+T_{0})}|\leq\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l+1}}
. Ignoring constants, this condition is satisfied when

\left(\frac{\sum_{m}d_{m}}{n}\right)^{-2\alpha_{l+1}}\left(\frac{\sum_{m}d_{m}}{n}\right)^{\alpha_{l}+\frac{1}{2}}=\left(\frac{\sum_{m}d_{m}}{n}\right)^{\alpha_{l+1}}.

This implies that
\alpha_{l+1}=\frac{1}{3}\alpha_{l}+\frac{1}{6}
. Next, we show that for any initial greater than
\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{0}=\sqrt{2}\sigma
, with enough steps,
|\omega^{(t)}|
will become smaller than
\sqrt{2}\sigma
. Therefore, for the outer bound of each annulus, we let
\alpha_{0}=0
and
\alpha_{l+1}=\frac{1}{3}\alpha_{l}+\frac{1}{6}
. Note that

\alpha_{l+1}=\frac{1}{3}\alpha_{l}+\frac{1}{6}=\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{1}{3}\alpha_{l-1}+\frac{1}{6}\right)+\frac{1}{6}=\cdots=\frac{1}{3^{l+1}}\alpha_{0}+\frac{1}{6}\frac{1-1/3^{l+1}}{1-1/3}=\frac{1}{4}(1-1/3^{l+1}).

This implies that for any
\alpha\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
, we have
\alpha_{l}\geq\frac{1}{4}-\alpha
for
l\geq\frac{\log(1/(4\alpha))}{\log(3)}
. Define
l_{\alpha}=\frac{\log(1/(4\alpha))}{\log(3)}+1
, we have
\alpha_{l_{\alpha}-1}\geq\frac{1}{4}-\alpha
.

Step 3:
In this step, we want to show that the updated parameter will converge into the next small annulus until
|\omega^{(t)}|=O\left(\left(\frac{\sum_{m}d_{m}}{n}\right)^{1/4-\alpha}\right)
for any
\alpha\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
. Specifically, in the first epoch, given
|\omega^{(t)}|\in[\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{0}},|\omega^{(0)}|]
, we need to show that, with enough steps, it will be smaller than
\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{0}}
. In the following epochs, given
|\omega^{(t)}|\in[\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l+1}},\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l}}]
, we need to show that, with enough steps, it will be smaller than
\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l+1}}
until
l\leq l_{\alpha}-2
. Before we introduce the details of the proof, we define
t_{0}
be the total iteration number in the first epoch, and
t_{l}
be the total iteration number in the
(l+1)
th epoch. Let
T_{l^{\prime}}=\sum_{l=0}^{l^{\prime}}t_{l}
be the total iteration number to reach at the
(l+1)
th epoch.

First epoch:
Given
|\omega^{(t)}|\in[\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{0}},|\omega^{(0)}|]
, we can get that

|\omega^{(t+1)}|\leq|\omega^{(t+1)}-\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|+|\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(t+1)}]|\leq c\sigma|\omega^{(0)}|\sqrt{\zeta}+\gamma_{p}(\omega^{(t)})|\omega^{(t)}|.

(A26)

The last inequality is the direct result of Lemma
9b
and Lemma
10b
. Similar as (
A22
), we have

\gamma_{p}(\omega^{(t)})=p+\frac{1-p}{1+\frac{(\omega^{(t)})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\leq p+\frac{1-p}{2}\leq\underbrace{1-\frac{1-p}{2}}_{\bar{\gamma}_{0}}.

(A27)

Recursing (
A26
) from
t=0
up to
t=T_{0}
, and using the fact that
\gamma_{p}(\omega^{(t)})\leq\bar{\gamma}_{0}
in this epoch, we find that

\displaystyle|\omega^{(T_{0})}|

\displaystyle\leq c\sigma\cdot|\omega^{(0)}|(1+\bar{\gamma}_{0}+\cdots+\bar{\gamma}_{0}^{T_{0}-1})+\bar{\gamma}_{0}^{T_{0}}|\omega^{(0)}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c\sigma|\omega^{(0)}|}{1-\bar{\gamma}_{0}}+\bar{\gamma}_{0}^{T_{0}}|\omega^{(0)}|.

Letting
T_{0}=\left\lceil\frac{2}{1-p}\log\left(\frac{|\omega^{(0)}|}{\sqrt{2\zeta}\sigma}\right)\right\rceil
and

n\geq\left(\frac{2c\sigma|\omega^{(0)}|}{1-p}+1\right)^{2}\sigma^{2}\left(\sum_{m}d_{m}+\log((2l_{\alpha}+1)/\delta)\right),

(A28)

we obtain that

|\omega^{(T_{0})}|\leq\left(\frac{2c\sigma|\omega^{(0)}|}{1-p}+1\right)\sqrt{2}\sigma\sqrt{\zeta}\leq\sqrt{2}\sigma.

Here
\lceil x\rceil
denotes the smallest integer greater than or equal to
x
.

Other epoch:
Given
|\omega^{(t)}|\in[\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l+1}},\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l}}]
, by (
A22
), we have

\gamma_{p}(\omega^{(t)})=p+\frac{1-p}{1+\frac{(\omega^{(t)})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\leq\underbrace{1-\frac{1-p}{2}\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}}_{\bar{\gamma}_{l}},

for all
t\in\{T_{l},\ldots,T_{l+1}-1\}
. Based on this, this proof can be divided into two parts. In the first part, we show that

|\omega^{(T_{l}+\lceil t_{l+1}/2\rceil)}|\leq c_{2}\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l+1}},

(A29)

where
c_{2}=2c\sigma/(1-p)+1
is a positive constant. In the second part, we can get that

|\omega^{(T_{l+1})}|\leq\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l+1}}.

(A30)

For (
A29
), it can be obtained by

\displaystyle|\omega^{(T_{l}+T)}|

\displaystyle\leq|\omega^{(T_{l}+T)}-\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(T_{l}+T)}]|+|\mathbb{E}[\omega^{(T_{l}+T)}]|

(A31)

\displaystyle\leq c\sigma\cdot\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l}}\sqrt{\zeta}+\bar{\gamma}_{l}|\omega^{(T_{l}+T-1)}|

\displaystyle\leq c\sigma\cdot\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l}}\sqrt{\zeta}(1+\bar{\gamma}_{l}+\cdots\bar{\gamma}_{T-1})+\bar{\gamma}_{l}^{T}|\omega^{(T_{l})}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2c\sigma\cdot\sqrt{2}\sigma}{1-p}\zeta^{\alpha_{l}+\frac{1}{2}-2\alpha_{l+1}}+e^{-T\frac{(1-p)\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}}{2}}\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha_{l}+\frac{1}{2}-2\alpha_{l+1}}\left(\frac{2c\sigma}{1-p}+1\right)=c_{2}\sqrt{2}\sigma\zeta^{\alpha^{l+1}},

where
c_{2}=\frac{2c\sigma}{1-p}+1
.
The third inequality follows the definition of
\bar{\gamma}_{l}
and (
A23
). The last inequality holds by
\alpha_{l}+\frac{1}{2}-2\alpha_{l+1}=\alpha_{l+1}
and
T\geq\frac{1-4\alpha_{l+1}}{(1-p)\zeta^{2\alpha_{l+1}}}\log(1/\zeta)
. Similar as (
A31
), staring at time
T_{l}+\lceil t_{l}/2\rceil
, we get that
